     1→<% content_for(:title) { "Getting Started — Sandcastle" } %>
     2→
     3→<div class="max-w-3xl mx-auto px-4 py-8">
     4→  <h1 class="text-3xl font-bold text-gray-900 mb-2">Getting Started</h1>
     5→  <p class="text-gray-500 mb-8">Everything you need to go from zero to a running sandbox.</p>
     6→
     7→  <% host = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_HOST", "sandcastle.example.com") %>
     8→
     9→  <%# ── Install ── %>
    10→  <section class="mb-10">
    11→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">1. Install the CLI</h2>
    12→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
    13→      Download the latest release from
    14→      <%= link_to "GitHub Releases", "https://github.com/thieso2/Sandcastle/releases/latest", target: "_blank", class: "text-blue-600 hover:text-blue-800 underline" %>,
    15→      then extract it:
    16→    </p>
    17→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>tar xzf sandcastle_*.tar.gz
    18→sudo mv sandcastle /usr/local/bin/</code></pre>
    19→  </section>
    20→
    21→  <%# ── Login ── %>
    22→  <section class="mb-10">
    23→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">2. Log in</h2>
    24→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">Point the CLI at this server and authenticate via your browser:</p>
    25→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle login https://<%= host %></code></pre>
    26→  </section>
    27→
    28→  <%# ── Tailscale ── %>
    29→  <section class="mb-10">
    30→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">3. Enable Tailscale <span class="text-sm font-normal text-gray-500">(recommended)</span></h2>
    31→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
    32→      Each sandbox gets its own Tailscale IP so you can reach services directly — no port forwarding needed.
    33→    </p>
    34→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Interactive login — opens a browser URL to authenticate
    35→sandcastle tailscale enable
    36→
    37→# Or use an auth key for headless setups
    38→sandcastle tailscale enable --auth-key tskey-auth-...</code></pre>
    39→    <p class="text-gray-500 text-sm mt-2">After authenticating, approve the advertised subnet routes in the Tailscale admin console.</p>
    40→  </section>
    41→
    42→  <%# ── Create ── %>
    43→  <section class="mb-10">
    44→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">4. Create a sandbox</h2>
    45→
    46→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3"><strong>Web UI</strong> — click the "Create Sandcastle" button on your dashboard to create a sandbox with all available options:</p>
    47→    <ul class="list-disc list-inside text-gray-700 ml-4 space-y-1 text-sm">
    48→      <li><strong>Name</strong> — unique identifier for your sandbox</li>
    49→      <li><strong>Container Image</strong> — custom image or default</li>
    50→      <li><strong>Snapshot</strong> — restore from an existing snapshot</li>
    51→      <li><strong>Persistent Volume</strong> — keep /workspace across recreations</li>
    52→      <li><strong>Mount Home</strong> — persistent home directory across all sandboxes</li>
    53→      <li><strong>Data Path</strong> — mount user data (or subpath) to /data</li>
    54→      <li><strong>Tailscale</strong> — connect to your Tailscale network</li>
    55→      <li><strong>Docker daemon</strong> — enable/disable Docker-in-Docker</li>
    56→      <li><strong>Temporary</strong> — auto-remove when you disconnect</li>
    57→    </ul>
    58→
    59→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3 mt-4"><strong>Or use the CLI</strong> for quick creation:</p>
    60→
    61→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3"><strong>Quick throwaway sandbox</strong> — deleted when you disconnect:</p>
    62→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle create scratch --rm</code></pre>
    63→
    64→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3 mt-4"><strong>Persistent sandbox</strong> — keeps your home directory and data across restarts:</p>
    65→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle create my-dev --home --data</code></pre>
    66→
    67→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3 mt-4"><strong>Lightweight sandbox</strong> — no Docker daemon or VNC (faster startup):</p>
    68→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle create lean --no-docker --no-vnc</code></pre>
    69→
    70→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-4 text-sm text-blue-800">
    71→      <strong>Tip:</strong> Make these flags the default with config or env vars:<br>
    72→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">sandcastle config set mount_home true</code><br>
    73→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">sandcastle config set data_path .</code><br>
    74→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">sandcastle config set vnc false</code><br>
    75→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">sandcastle config set docker false</code><br>
    76→      <span class="text-blue-600 text-xs mt-1 block">Or via env: <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">export SANDCASTLE_HOME=1 SANDCASTLE_DATA=. SANDCASTLE_VNC=false</code></span>
    77→    </div>
    78→  </section>
    79→
    80→  <%# ── Connect ── %>
    81→  <section class="mb-10">
    82→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">5. Connect</h2>
    83→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">Two ways to get a shell:</p>
    84→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># tmux session (reconnectable, auto-starts stopped sandboxes)
    85→sandcastle connect my-dev
    86→
    87→# Plain SSH
    88→sandcastle ssh my-dev</code></pre>
    89→
    90→    <h3 class="text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mt-4 mb-2">Mosh (persistent, roaming sessions)</h3>
    91→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
    92→      Use <a href="https://mosh.org" target="_blank" class="text-blue-600 hover:text-blue-800 underline">mosh</a>
    93→      for a connection that survives network changes and high-latency links. Mosh is pre-installed in every sandbox.
    94→    </p>
    95→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle connect --mosh my-dev</code></pre>
    96→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-3 text-sm text-blue-800">
    97→      <strong>Requirements:</strong> <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">mosh</code> must be installed on your local machine
    98→      (<code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">brew install mosh</code> on macOS,
    99→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">apt install mosh</code> on Debian/Ubuntu).
   100→      Mosh uses UDP ports 60000–61000 — if your sandbox is reachable only via SSH tunneling, use
   101→      <a href="#tailscale" class="underline">Tailscale</a> instead (it handles UDP natively).
   102→    </div>
   103→  </section>
   104→
   105→  <%# ── Web Terminal ── %>
   106→  <section class="mb-10">
   107→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">6. Web terminal</h2>
   108→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   109→      Click <strong>tmux</strong> or <strong>shell</strong> on any running sandbox to open a browser-based terminal.
   110→      No SSH client needed — just a web browser.
   111→    </p>
   112→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 text-sm text-blue-800">
   113→      <strong>How it works:</strong> ttyd runs inside each sandbox container, exposing two terminal endpoints:
   114→      <strong>tmux</strong> (persistent, re-attachable session shared across browser tabs) and
   115→      <strong>shell</strong> (a plain login shell).
   116→      Both are protected by your existing login — no extra passwords required.
   117→    </div>
   118→  </section>
   119→
   120→  <%# ── VNC ── %>
   121→  <section class="mb-10">
   122→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">7. VNC</h2>
   123→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   124→      Click the <strong>VNC</strong> button on any running sandbox to access Google Chrome with a graphical desktop environment.
   125→      Perfect for browser automation, visual testing, or running GUI applications.
   126→    </p>
   127→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 text-sm text-blue-800 mb-3">
   128→      <strong>How it works:</strong> Each sandbox includes Google Chrome, Xvfb (virtual X server), and x11vnc.
   129→      Clicking VNC launches a noVNC container that connects to the sandbox's VNC server, giving you a full graphical browser in your web browser.
   130→    </div>
   131→    <div class="bg-green-50 border border-green-200 rounded-lg p-4 text-sm text-green-800">
   132→      <strong>Chrome profile persistence:</strong> Go to Settings → VNC to enable Chrome profile persistence.
   133→      When enabled, your Chrome extensions, bookmarks, and settings are saved and persist across sandbox recreations.
   134→      If you use <strong>Mount Home</strong>, profiles are stored in <code class="bg-green-100 px-1 rounded">~/.config/google-chrome</code> alongside all your other home directory files.
   135→      Without Mount Home, profiles are stored separately in a dedicated directory.
   136→    </div>
   137→  </section>
   138→
   139→  <%# ── Manage ── %>
   140→  <section class="mb-10">
   141→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">8. Manage sandboxes</h2>
   142→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle list              # Show all your sandboxes
   143→sandcastle stop my-dev       # Stop a running sandbox
   144→sandcastle start my-dev      # Start a stopped sandbox
   145→sandcastle rename my-dev new # Rename a sandbox
   146→sandcastle delete my-dev     # Archive sandbox (soft-delete, data kept)
   147→
   148→# Copy files to/from a sandbox
   149→sandcastle cp file.txt my-dev:~/           # local → sandbox
   150→sandcastle cp my-dev:~/data.csv .          # sandbox → local
   151→sandcastle cp -r my-dev:~/project ./       # recursive</code></pre>
   152→
   153→    <h3 class="text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mt-4 mb-2">Archive &amp; restore</h3>
   154→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   155→      When you delete a sandbox it is <strong>archived</strong> rather than immediately purged.
   156→      Archived sandboxes have no running container but their data is preserved on disk.
   157→      After the retention period (default: 30 days) a background job permanently deletes them.
   158→      Admins can adjust the system-wide retention on the Settings page, and each user can override it
   159→      in their profile settings.
   160→    </p>
   161→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># List archived sandboxes
   162→sandcastle list --archived
   163→
   164→# Restore an archived sandbox (recreates and starts the container)
   165→sandcastle unarchive my-dev
   166→
   167→# Permanently delete an archived sandbox immediately, bypassing the retention period
   168→sandcastle purge my-dev</code></pre>
   169→    <p class="text-gray-500 text-sm mt-2">
   170→      Archived sandboxes also appear as a collapsible section on your dashboard with a <strong>Restore</strong> button.
   171→      The sandbox name is freed immediately on archive, so you can create a new sandbox with the same name.
   172→    </p>
   173→  </section>
   174→
   175→  <%# ── Routes ── %>
   176→  <section class="mb-10">
   177→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">9. Routes</h2>
   178→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   179→      Expose services running inside your sandbox to the outside world. Two modes are supported:
   180→    </p>
   181→
   182→    <h3 class="text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mb-2">HTTP routes (layer 7, custom domain + TLS)</h3>
   183→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   184→      Route a custom domain to a port inside the sandbox. Traefik terminates TLS automatically.
   185→    </p>
   186→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Add an HTTP route — app.example.com → container port 8080 (default)
   187→sandcastle route add myapp app.example.com
   188→
   189→# Add an HTTP route on a custom container port
   190→sandcastle route add myapp api.example.com 3000</code></pre>
   191→    <p class="text-gray-500 text-sm mt-2 mb-4">Point a DNS A/CNAME record at <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded"><%= host %></code> before adding the route. TLS certificates are provisioned automatically.</p>
   192→
   193→    <h3 class="text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mb-2">TCP routes (layer 4, raw port forward)</h3>
   194→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   195→      Forward a public TCP port (3000–3099) directly to a container port. Useful for databases,
   196→      message brokers, or any non-HTTP service.
   197→    </p>
   198→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Forward a public port to container port 5432 (e.g. Postgres)
   199→sandcastle route add myapp --tcp 5432
   200→# Output: TCP port 3042 → container port 5432</code></pre>
   201→    <p class="text-gray-500 text-sm mt-2 mb-4">A public port from the pool is assigned automatically. Connect with <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded"><%= host %>:&lt;public-port&gt;</code>.</p>
   202→
   203→    <h3 class="text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mb-2">Managing routes</h3>
   204→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># List all routes for a sandbox (shows ID, mode, domain/port, container port)
   205→sandcastle route list myapp
   206→
   207→# Remove an HTTP route by domain
   208→sandcastle route delete myapp app.example.com
   209→
   210→# Remove any route by ID (use the ID from route list)
   211→sandcastle route delete myapp --id 42</code></pre>
   212→    <p class="text-gray-500 text-sm mt-2">Routes can also be managed from the sandbox detail page in the web UI.</p>
   213→  </section>
   214→
   215→  <%# ── Snapshots ── %>
   216→  <section class="mb-10">
   217→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">10. Snapshots</h2>
   218→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   219→      Save and restore the full state of a sandbox. Snapshots are composite — they capture
   220→      the container filesystem (via <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded">docker commit</code>)
   221→      and, when on a BTRFS filesystem, also the home directory, data directory, and workspace volume.
   222→    </p>
   223→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Create a snapshot (all available layers)
   224→sandcastle snapshot create my-dev before-migration
   225→
   226→# Create with a description and specific layers
   227→sandcastle snapshot create my-dev checkpoint --label "before big refactor" --layers container,home
   228→
   229→# List all snapshots
   230→sandcastle snapshot list
   231→
   232→# Show details of a specific snapshot
   233→sandcastle snapshot show before-migration
   234→
   235→# Restore a snapshot into an existing sandbox (all layers)
   236→sandcastle snapshot restore my-dev before-migration
   237→
   238→# Restore only the container layer
   239→sandcastle snapshot restore my-dev before-migration --layers container
   240→
   241→# Create a new sandbox from a snapshot (all layers)
   242→sandcastle create my-dev-v2 --from-snapshot before-migration
   243→
   244→# Clone only the container layer into a new sandbox
   245→sandcastle create my-dev-v2 --from-snapshot before-migration --restore-layers container
   246→
   247→# Delete a snapshot
   248→sandcastle snapshot delete before-migration</code></pre>
   249→    <p class="text-gray-700 mt-3 mb-1">
   250→      <strong>Web UI:</strong> Each sandbox has a detail page (click the sandbox name) with a Snapshots tab
   251→      for creating, viewing, and deleting snapshots. The global
   252→      <%= link_to "Snapshots page", snapshots_path, class: "text-blue-600 hover:text-blue-800 underline" %>
   253→      shows all snapshots across all your sandboxes with clone-to-new-sandbox support.
   254→    </p>
   255→    <% unless (BtrfsHelper.btrfs? rescue false) %>
   256→      <div class="bg-yellow-50 border border-yellow-200 rounded-lg p-4 text-sm text-yellow-800 mt-3">
   257→        <strong>Note:</strong> BTRFS not detected on this host. Only the container layer is snapshotted.
   258→        Home, data, and workspace layers require BTRFS.
   259→      </div>
   260→    <% end %>
   261→  </section>
   262→
   263→  <%# ── Settings ── %>
   264→  <section class="mb-10">
   265→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">11. User settings</h2>
   266→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   267→      Click <strong>Settings</strong> in the navbar to manage your account preferences:
   268→    </p>
   269→    <ul class="list-disc list-inside text-gray-700 ml-4 space-y-1 text-sm">
   270→      <li><strong>Profile</strong> — Update your email address and change your password</li>
   271→      <li><strong>Tailscale</strong> — View connection status and toggle auto-connect for new sandboxes</li>
   272→      <li><strong>Sandbox Archive</strong> — Set your personal archive retention period (overrides the system default)</li>
   273→      <li><strong>VNC</strong> — Toggle Chrome profile persistence across sandbox recreations</li>
   274→      <li><strong>API Tokens</strong> — Create, list, and revoke multiple named API tokens for different devices or applications</li>
   275→    </ul>
   276→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-4 text-sm text-blue-800">
   277→      <strong>Using API tokens:</strong> Create tokens for different use cases (laptop, CI/CD, mobile).<br>
   278→      <strong>Via Web UI:</strong> Go to Settings → click "New Token" → give it a name → copy the token<br>
   279→      <strong>Via CLI:</strong> Run <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">sandcastle token create laptop</code> to create a named token<br>
   280→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">sandcastle token list</code> to view all your tokens<br>
   281→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">sandcastle token revoke &lt;id&gt;</code> to revoke a specific token
   282→    </div>
   283→  </section>
   284→
   285→  <%# ── Inviting Users (Admin) ── %>
   286→  <section class="mb-10">
   287→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">12. Inviting users <span class="text-sm font-normal text-gray-500">(admin)</span></h2>
   288→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   289→      Admins can invite new users via email. Each invite generates a unique, one-time registration link.
   290→      New users click the link to choose a username and set a password — no pre-created account required.
   291→    </p>
   292→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3"><strong>To send an invite:</strong></p>
   293→    <ol class="list-decimal list-inside text-gray-700 ml-4 space-y-1 text-sm mb-4">
   294→      <li>Go to <strong>Admin → Invites</strong></li>
   295→      <li>Click <strong>Invite User</strong></li>
   296→      <li>Enter the invitee's email address and an optional personal message</li>
   297→      <li>Click <strong>Send Invite</strong> — the invitee will receive an email with a registration link</li>
   298→    </ol>
   299→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3"><strong>Invite lifecycle:</strong></p>
   300→    <ul class="list-disc list-inside text-gray-700 ml-4 space-y-1 text-sm mb-3">
   301→      <li><strong>Pending</strong> — sent but not yet accepted; link is still valid</li>
   302→      <li><strong>Accepted</strong> — user has registered; link is invalidated</li>
   303→      <li><strong>Expired</strong> — link expired (default: 7 days) before being accepted</li>
   304→    </ul>
   305→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   306→      Pending invites can be revoked from the Invites admin panel at any time.
   307→      Once revoked, the registration link immediately becomes invalid.
   308→    </p>
   309→  </section>
   310→
   311→  <%# ── Custom SSL Certificates ── %>
   312→  <section class="mb-10">
   313→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">13. Custom SSL certificates <span class="text-sm font-normal text-gray-500">(advanced)</span></h2>
   314→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   315→      For internal networks, you can configure alternative hostnames with custom SSL certificates to avoid browser warnings.
   316→    </p>
   317→
   318→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3"><strong>Generate certificates with mkcert:</strong></p>
   319→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Install mkcert (macOS example)
   320→brew install mkcert
   321→mkcert -install
   322→
   323→# Generate certificate for your domains
   324→mkcert sandcastle.internal sc.local</code></pre>
   325→
   326→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3 mt-4"><strong>Configure Sandcastle:</strong></p>
   327→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">Add these variables to your <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded">sandcastle.env</code> before installation:</p>
   328→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>SANDCASTLE_ALT_HOSTNAMES=sandcastle.internal,sc.local
   329→REDACTED.internal+1.pem
   330→REDACTED.internal+1-key.pem
   331→SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE=selfsigned</code></pre>
   332→
   333→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3 mt-4">The installer will copy the certificates to <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded">$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/traefik/certs/</code> and configure Traefik to serve them for the specified hostnames.</p>
   334→
   335→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-4 text-sm text-blue-800">
   336→      <strong>Note:</strong> You'll need to add DNS entries (or edit <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">/etc/hosts</code>) to point your alternative hostnames to the Sandcastle server IP.
   337→    </div>
   338→  </section>
   339→
   340→  <%# ── CLI Preferences ── %>
   341→  <section class="mb-10">
   342→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">14. CLI preferences</h2>
   343→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   344→      Persist connection preferences in <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded">~/.sandcastle/config.yaml</code>.
   345→      Environment variables override the file (useful with
   346→      <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded">mise</code> or <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded">direnv</code>).
   347→    </p>
   348→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Use mosh instead of SSH by default
   349→sandcastle config set connect_protocol mosh
   350→
   351→# Disable automatic tmux session attachment
   352→sandcastle config set use_tmux false
   353→
   354→# Always mount persistent home directory on create
   355→sandcastle config set mount_home true
   356→
   357→# Always mount user data directory on create
   358→sandcastle config set data_path .
   359→
   360→# Disable VNC by default
   361→sandcastle config set vnc false
   362→
   363→# Disable Docker (DinD) by default
   364→sandcastle config set docker false
   365→
   366→# Show effective settings (merged from config file + env vars)
   367→sandcastle config show</code></pre>
   368→
   369→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-2 mt-4">Environment variable overrides (highest priority):</p>
   370→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>SANDCASTLE_CONNECT_PROTOCOL=mosh  # "ssh" (default) | "mosh"
   371→SANDCASTLE_USE_TMUX=false         # "true" (default) | "false"
   372→SANDCASTLE_SSH_EXTRA_ARGS="-v"    # extra flags appended to ssh/mosh
   373→SANDCASTLE_HOME=1                 # mount persistent home on create
   374→SANDCASTLE_DATA=.                 # mount user data dir on create
   375→SANDCASTLE_VNC=false              # disable VNC on create
   376→SANDCASTLE_DOCKER=false           # disable Docker (DinD) on create</code></pre>
   377→
   378→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-2 mt-4">Example: per-project override with mise:</p>
   379→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># .mise.toml
   380→[env]
   381→SANDCASTLE_CONNECT_PROTOCOL = "mosh"
   382→SANDCASTLE_USE_TMUX = "false"</code></pre>
   383→  </section>
   384→
   385→  <section class="mb-8">
   386→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">15. Short aliases</h2>
   387→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
   388→      Common commands have 1-2 letter shortcuts:
   389→    </p>
   390→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle cr        # create (temp sandbox)
   391→sandcastle cr dev    # create dev
   392→sandcastle c dev     # connect dev
   393→sandcastle s dev     # ssh dev
   394→sandcastle x dev -- cmd  # exec
   395→sandcastle ls        # list
   396→sandcastle rm dev    # delete dev
   397→sandcastle mv dev new  # rename dev → new
   398→sandcastle up dev    # start dev
   399→sandcastle dn dev    # stop dev
   400→sandcastle cp f dev:~/ # scp file to sandbox
   401→sandcastle i         # info
   402→sandcastle st        # status
   403→sandcastle snap      # snapshot
   404→sandcastle ts        # tailscale</code></pre>
   405→  </section>
   406→
   407→  <div class="border-t border-gray-200 pt-6 text-center">
   408→    <p class="text-sm text-gray-500">
   409→      Run <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded">sandcastle --help</code> for the full command reference.
   410→    </p>

Connect</h2>\n    <p class=\"text-gray-700 mb-3\">Two ways to get a shell:</p>\n    <pre class=\"bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed\"><code># tmux session (reconnectable, auto-starts stopped sandboxes)\nsandcastle connect my-dev\n\n# Plain SSH\nsandcastle ssh my-dev</code></pre>\n\n    <h3 class=\"text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mt-4 mb-2\">Mosh (persistent, roaming sessions)</h3>\n    <p class=\"text-gray-700 mb-3\">\n      Use <a href=\"https://mosh.org\" target=\"_blank\" class=\"text-blue-600 hover:text-blue-800 underline\">mosh</a>\n      for a connection that survives network changes and high-latency links. Mosh is pre-installed in every sandbox.\n    </p>\n    <pre class=\"bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed\"><code>sandcastle connect --mosh my-dev</code></pre>\n    <div class=\"bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-3 text-sm text-blue-800\">\n      <strong>Requirements:</strong> <code class=\"bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded\">mosh</code> must be installed on your local machine\n      (<code class=\"bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded\">brew install mosh</code> on macOS,\n      <code class=\"bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded\">apt install mosh</code> on Debian/Ubuntu).\n      Mosh uses UDP ports 60000–61000 — if your sandbox is reachable only via SSH tunneling, use\n      <a href=\"#tailscale\" class=\"underline\">Tailscale</a> instead (it handles UDP natively).\n    </div>\n  </section>",
  "new_string": "  <%# ── Connect ── %>\n  <section class=\"mb-10\">\n    <h2 class=\"text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3\">5. Connect</h2>\n    <p class=\"text-gray-700 mb-3\">Two ways to get a shell:</p>\n    <pre class=\"bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed\"><code># tmux session (reconnectable, auto-starts stopped sandboxes)\nsandcastle connect my-dev\n\n# Plain SSH (no tmux)\nsandcastle ssh my-dev\n\n# Pass extra SSH options after --\nsandcastle connect my-dev -- -L 8080:localhost:8080\nsandcastle ssh my-dev -- -D 1080</code></pre>\n\n    <h3 class=\"text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mt-4 mb-2\">SSH agent forwarding</h3>\n    <p class=\"text-gray-700 mb-3\">\n      The CLI automatically forwards your local SSH agent (<code class=\"bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded\">-A</code>)\n      so you can use your SSH keys (e.g. for Git) inside the sandbox without copying them.\n      This works with the default SSH protocol.\n    </p>\n\n    <h3 class=\"text-base font-semibold text-gray-800 mt-4 mb-2\">Mosh (persistent, roaming sessions)</h3>\n    <p class=\"text-gray-700 mb-3\">\n      Use <a href=\"https://mosh.org\" target=\"_blank\" class=\"text-blue-600 hover:text-blue-800 underline\">mosh</a>\n      for a connection that survives network changes and high-latency links. Mosh is pre-installed in every sandbox.\n    </p>\n    <pre class=\"bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed\"><code>sandcastle connect --mosh my-dev</code></pre>\n    <div class=\"bg-yellow-50 border border-yellow-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-3 text-sm text-yellow-800\">\n      <strong>Warning:</strong> Mosh does <strong>not</strong> support SSH agent forwarding.\n      Mosh drops the SSH connection after bootstrap and switches to UDP, which has no agent forwarding support.\n      Your <code class=\"bg-yellow-100 px-1 rounded\">ssh-add -l</code> keys will not be available inside the sandbox.\n      If you need agent forwarding (e.g. for Git over SSH), use the default SSH protocol instead.\n    </div>\n    <div class=\"bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-3 text-sm text-blue-800\">\n      <strong>Requirements:</strong> <code class=\"bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded\">mosh</code> must be installed on your local machine\n      (<code class=\"bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded\">brew install mosh</code> on macOS,\n      <code class=\"bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded\">apt install mosh</code> on Debian/Ubuntu).\n      Mosh uses UDP ports 60000–61000 — if your sandbox is reachable only via SSH tunneling, use\n      <a href=\"#tailscale\" class=\"underline\">Tailscale</a> instead (it handles UDP natively).\n    </div>\n  </section>"
}

  "new_string": "var connectMoshFlag string\n\nfunc init() {\n\trootCmd.AddCommand(connectCmd)\n\trootCmd.AddCommand(sshCmd)\n\tconnectCmd.Flags().StringVar(&connectMoshFlag, \"mosh\", \"\", \"Use mosh (yes|no). }

> AGENT

Released as **v0.8.63**.

Usage:
```
sc c mybox --mosh        # use mosh (= --mosh=yes)
sc c mybox --mosh=no     # force SSH
sc c mybox               # default (SSH)
sc ssh mybox --mosh      # also works on ssh command
```

When mosh is used, prints: `Warning: mosh does not support SSH agent forwarding. Use --mosh=no if you need ssh-add keys inside the sandbox.`
